# Data Structures and Algorithms — syllabus

80 syllabus section(s) for this course in the archive, newest batch first. Text comes from the PDF (OCR for scanned pages; check figures against the source). All programmes' syllabi: [`syllabi/`](../../syllabi/README.md).

## B TECH · Artificial Intelligence · AY 2026-27 · Sem 3

- Course as printed: Data Structures and Algorithms (code 702CO0C083)
- Source: `00_Original_Downloads/SYLLABUS/B TECH/11755__2ND YEAR.zip :: ARTIFICIAL INTELLIGENCE/SEM III/B_Tech_Artificial_Intelligence__2nd_Year__Sem-III__A_Y_2026-27_vYlWgpIuJh.pdf`, page 20-23

````text
Program: B Tech (CE, DS, AI, AIML, AIDS, IT, CSECS, CSEDS, CSBS, Semester: III
CS)
MBA Tech (CE, IT, DS, AI)
Course: Data Structures and Algorithms   Code: 702CO0C083
Teaching Scheme   Evaluation Scheme
Lecture   Practical   Tutorial   Internal Continuous   Term End
(Hours   (Hours per (Hours per   Credit   Assessment (ICA)   Examinations (TEE)
per week)   week)   week)   (Marks - 50)   (Marks- 100)
3   0   0   3   Marks Scaled to 50   Marks Scaled to 50
Pre-requisite: Computational Thinking for Problem Solving
Course Objective
This course imparts knowledge of data structures and algorithms so as to identify and implement
appropriate data structure and determine the computational complexity of the given application
Course Outcomes-
After completion of the course, the students will be able to –
1.Apply fundamental data structure concepts and computational complexity (K3)
2.Distinguish between iterative and recursive approaches to problem-solving and implement
algorithms using both techniques (K4)
3.Apply linear data structure and its operations for the given application (K3)
4.Apply non-linear data structure and its operations for the given application (K3)
5.Apply the concept of hashing and hashing functions to create efficient data retrieval mechanisms (K3)
6.Analyze various sorting algorithms (K4)
Detailed Syllabus:
Unit   Description   Duration
1   Introduction   03
Introduction to data structure and its importance, Classification of data structures,
Representation of arrays in memory, Operations on arrays -Traversal, Insertion,
Deletion, Searching (Linear search, Binary search).
Abstract data type, Performance analysis- time and space complexity, Asymptotic   03
Notations.
2   Iteration and Recursion   04
Iterative and recursive algorithms (Towers of Hanoi, Euclid’s GCD algorithm,
Fibonacci series, etc)
3   Linear Data Structure - I   03
Introduction to Stacks, Operations on Stacks, Applications of stacks -
Expression conversion and evaluation (Polish notation), Balanced parenthesis
checker,
Signature
(Head of the Department)   AY 2026-27
SVKM’s NMIMS Deemed-to-be University
Mukesh Patel School of Technology Management and Engineering
Introduction to Queue, Operation on Queues, Linear queue Circular queue, Priority   03
queue, Application of Queues.
4   Linear Data Structures - I   04
Introduction to linked list, Representation of linked list in memory, Singly linked list
and its operations, Doubly Linked list and its operations
Linked list representation of Stack and Queues, Applications of linked list –   02
Polynomial Addition. Analysis of algorithms.
5   Hashing   03
Hashing: hashing function,
Collision handling by open addressing, chaining.   02
6   Non-Linear Data Structures- II   04
Introduction, Binary tree terminologies, Representation of Binary trees in memory,
Binary Tree traversal algorithms, Construction of Binary Tree from traversals,
Binary Search Tree: Insertion, Deletion.
AVL trees: Rotations, Insertion. Applications of tree data structure: Heapify, Heap   03
Sort, Huffman trees. Analysis of algorithms.
7   Non- Linear Data Structures - II   03
Introduction, Graph theory terminology, Representation of graph: Adjacency
Matrix, Adjacency List, Graph Traversal: Breadth first search, Depth first search.
Applications of Graphs (Problem Solving): Shortest path (Dijkstra’s algorithm),   03
Minimum Spanning Tree: Prim's and Kruskal's.
Analysis of algorithms.
8   Sorting   03
Insertion, Selection,
Merge Sort, Analysis of algorithms.   02
Total   45
Text Text Books Books
1. Seymour Lipschutz, “Data structures with C”, Schaum’s Outlines, 1st Edition, 2018. (Classic)
2. Reema Thareja, “Data Structures using C”, Oxford University Press, 2nd Edition, 2023
Reference Books:
1. Richard F. Gillberg, Behrouz A. Forouzen, “Data Structures – A Pseudo Approach with C”, Cengage
Publication, 2nd Edition 2004. (Classic)
2. Mark Allen Weiss, “Data Structures and Algorithm analysis in C++”, PHI, 4th Edition, 2013.
3. Thomas, H. Cormen, Charles E. Leiserson, Ronald L. Rivest, Clifford Stein, “Introduction to
Algorithms”, MIT Press, 3rd Edition 2009.
Signature
(Head of the Department)   AY 2026-27
SVKM’s NMIMS Deemed-to-be University
Mukesh Patel School of Technology Management and Engineering
4. Y. Langsam, M.J. Augenstein, A.M. Tenenbaum, “Data Structures using C and C++”, PHI 2nd Edition,
2015.
Onl Online Resources:
1. Morin, Pat. "Open Data Structures (in C++)." 2011, pag 264 (2012).
https://opendatastructures.org/ods-cpp.pdf
Data Structure Visualizations,
https://www.cs.usfca.edu/~galles/visualization/Algorithms.html
Signature
(Head of the Department)   AY 2026-27
SVKM’s NMIMS Deemed-to-be University
Mukesh Patel School of Technology Management and Engineering

CSBS, CS)
````

````text
Program: Master of Computer Applications (MCA)   Semester: I
Course: Data Structures and Algorithms   Code: 703CO0C006
Teaching Scheme   Evaluation Scheme
Lecture   Tutorial
Practical   Internal Continuous   Term End Examinations
(Hours   (Hours
(Hours per   Credit   Assessment (ICA)   (TEE)
per   per
week)   (Marks - 50)   (Marks- 100 )
week)   week)
3   2   0   4   Marks Scaled to 50   Marks Scaled to 50
Prerequisite: Programming for Problem Solving
Course Objective
This course imparts knowledge of data structures and algorithms so as to identify and implement
appropriate data structure and determine the computational complexity of the given application.
Course Outcomes
After completion of the course, students will be able to -
1.   Understand the concept of data structures and computational complexity
2.   Identify and implement appropriate linear data structure for the given problem.
3.   Identify and implement appropriate non-linear data structure for the given problem.
4.   Differentiate various searching and sorting algorithms.
Detailed Syllabus
Unit Description   Duration
1   Introduction   04
Introduction to data structure and its importance, Classification of data structures,
Basic operations., Abstract data type, Performance analysis- time and space
complexity, Asymptotic Notations.
2   Linear Data Structure I   10
Representation of arrays in memory, Operations on arrays -Traversal, Insertion,
Deletion. Introduction to Stacks, Operations on Stacks, Applications of stacks -
Expression conversion and evaluation (Polish notation), Balanced parenthesis
checker, Recursion, Introduction to Queue,
Operation on Queues, Linear queue Circular queue, Priority queue, Application
of Queues.
3   Linear Data Structure II   07
Introduction to linked list, Representation of linked list in memory, Singly linked
list and its operations, Introduction to Doubly Linked list Linked list
representation of Stack and Queues, Applications of linked list – Polynomial
Addition
4   Non-Linear Data Structures - I   10
Signature
(Head of the Department)   AY 2026-27
SVKM’s NMIMS University
Mukesh Patel School of Technology Management & Engineering
Introduction, Binary tree terminologies, Representation of Binary trees in
memory, Binary Tree traversal algorithms, Construction of Binary Tree from
traversals, Binary Search Tree: Insertion, Deletion, Applications of tree data
structure: Expression trees, Huffman trees.
5   Non- Linear Data Structures - II   06
Introduction, Graph theory terminology, Representation of graph: Adjacency
Matrix, Adjacency List, Graph Traversal: Breadth first search, Depth first search,
Applications of Graphs (Problem Solving): Shortest path (Dijkstra’s algorithm),
Minimum Spanning Tree.
6   Searching and Sorting   08
Linear Search, Binary Search, Selection Sort, Insertion sort, Merge sort,
Introduction to Hashing
Total   45
Text Books
1. Seymour Lipschutz, “Data structures with C”, Schaum’s Outlines, 1st Edition, 2017. ISBN-13 : 978-
0070701984.
2. Reema Thareja, “Data Structures using C”, Oxford University Press, 3rd Edition, 2023.
3. Y. Langsam, M.J. Augenstein, A.M. Tenenbaum, “Data Structures using C and C++”, PHI 2nd
Edition, Pearson Education, 2015. ISBN 9789332549319
Reference Books
1. Richard F. Gillberg, Behrouz A. Forouzen, “Data Structures – A Pseudo Approach with C”, Cengage
Publication, 2nd Edition 2004. (Classic)
2. Mark Allen Weiss, “Data Structures and Algorithm analysis in C++”, PHI, 4th Edition, 2013. ISBN-13:
9780132847377 Data Structures and Algorithm Analysis in C++ (pearson.com)
3.Thomas, H. Cormen, Charles E. Leiserson, Ronald L. Rivest, Clifford Stein, “Introduction to
Algorithms”, MIT Press, 3rd Edition 2009.
Laboratory Work
8 to 10 experiments (and a practicum where applicable) based on the syllabus.
````

````text
4   Non-Linear Data Structures - I   10
Introduction, Binary tree terminologies, Representation of Binary trees in
memory, Binary Tree traversal algorithms, Construction of Binary Tree from
traversals, Binary Search Tree: Insertion, Deletion, Applications of tree data
structure: Expression trees, Huffman trees.
5   Non- Linear Data Structures - II   06
Introduction, Graph theory terminology, Representation of graph: Adjacency
Matrix, Adjacency List, Graph Traversal: Breadth first search, Depth first search,
Applications of Graphs (Problem Solving): Shortest path (Dijkstra’s algorithm),
Minimum Spanning Tree.
6   Searching and Sorting   08
Linear Search, Binary Search, Selection Sort, Insertion sort, Merge sort,
Introduction to Hashing
Total   45
Text Books
1. Seymour Lipschutz, “Data structures with C”, Schaum’s Outlines, 1st Edition, 2017. ISBN-13 : 978-
0070701984.
2. Reema Thareja, “Data Structures using C”, Oxford University Press, 3rd Edition, 2023.
3. Y. Langsam, M.J. Augenstein, A.M. Tenenbaum, “Data Structures using C and C++”, PHI 2nd
Edition, Pearson Education, 2015. ISBN 9789332549319
Reference Books
1. Richard F. Gillberg, Behrouz A. Forouzen, “Data Structures – A Pseudo Approach with C”, Cengage
Publication, 2nd Edition 2004. (Classic)
2. Mark Allen Weiss, “Data Structures and Algorithm analysis in C++”, PHI, 4th Edition, 2013. ISBN-13:
9780132847377 Data Structures and Algorithm Analysis in C++ (pearson.com)
3.Thomas, H. Cormen, Charles E. Leiserson, Ronald L. Rivest, Clifford Stein, “Introduction to
Algorithms”, MIT Press, 3rd Edition 2009.
Laboratory Work
8 to 10 experiments (and a practicum where applicable) based on the syllabus.
Signature
(Head of the Department)   AY 2025-26
````

````text
Program: B Tech (CE, DS, AI, AIML, AIDS, IT, CSECS, CSEDS, CSBS, Semester: III
CS)
MBA Tech (CE, IT, DS, AI)

Lecture   Practical   Tutorial   Internal Continuous   Term End

per week)   week)   week)   (Marks - 50)   (Marks- 100)

Pre-requisite: Computational Thinking for Problem Solving
Course Objective
This course imparts knowledge of data structures and algorithms so as to identify and implement
appropriate data structure and determine the computational complexity of the given application
Course Outcomes-
After completion of the course, the students will be able to –
1.Apply fundamental data structure concepts and computational complexity (K3)
2.Distinguish between iterative and recursive approaches to problem-solving and implement
algorithms using both techniques (K4)
3.Apply linear data structure and its operations for the given application (K3)
4.Apply non-linear data structure and its operations for the given application (K3)
5.Apply the concept of hashing and hashing functions to create efficient data retrieval mechanisms (K3)
6.Analyze various sorting algorithms (K4)

Deletion, Searching (Linear search, Binary search).

Expression conversion and evaluation (Polish notation), Balanced parenthesis
checker,
````

## B TECH · Data Science · AY 2024-25 · Sem 3

- Course as printed: Data Structures and Algorithms (code 702CO0C003)
- Source: `04_Syllabi_Archive/B TECH/2ND YEAR/COMPUTER ENGINEERING (CSDS) 311/SEM III/B Tech, CSE Data Science, Sem-III, A Y 2024-25.pdf`, page 16-18

````text
Program: B. Tech Computer Science and Engineering   Semester: III
(Data Science) – 311 (VT)
Course: Data Structures and Algorithms   Code: 702CO0C003
Teaching Scheme   Evaluation Scheme
Lecture   Practical   Tutorial   Internal Continuous   Term End
(Hours   (Hours
(Hours   Credit   Assessment (ICA)   Examinations (TEE)
per   per
week)   per week)   week)   (Marks - 50)   (Marks - 100)
2   2   0   3   Marks Scaled to 50   Marks Scaled to 50
Pre-requisite: Programming for Problem Solving, Object Oriented programming and design.
Course Objective
To impart the knowledge of data structures and algorithms so that students can identify and
implement appropriate data structure and determine the computational complexity of given
problem statement.
Course Outcomes
After completion of the course, the student will be able to -
1. Identify suitable data structure for particular type of problem to solve
2. Implement Linear Data Structures
3. Implement Non Linear Data Structure
4. Differentiate among various types of algorithms
Detailed Syllabus
Unit   Description   Duration
1   Introduction to Algorithm & Data Organization   05
Algorithm specification, Recursion, Performance analysis, Asymptotic
Notation - The Big-O, Omega and Theta notation, Data Abstraction, What is
Data structure? Its importance in programming, Ways to identify the suitable
data structure.
2   Linear Data Structure   08
Array, Stack, Queue, Circular Queue, Singly Linked List, Doubly Linked List,
Circular Linked List, Various Representations, Operations & Applications of
Linear Data Structures, Case study of real life problem to solve using linear
data structure.
3   Non-linear Data Structure   09
Trees (Binary Tree, Threaded Binary Tree, Binary Search Tree, B & B+ Tree,
AVL Tree, Splay Tree and
Graph: Basic Terminologies and Representations, Graphs (Directed,
Undirected), ,Graph search and traversal algorithms, Operations &
Applications of Non Linear Data Structures, Case study of real life problem to
solve using Nonlinear data structure.
4   Introduction to various Algorithms   08
Greedy Algorithm, Divide and Conquer, Backtracking, Searching, Sorting,
Graph algorithms, Genetic algorithm, Case study of real life problem to solve
using linear data structure.
Total   30
B. Tech CSE (DS) – 311 (VT)/SEM III/AY 2024-25/Page 16
SVKM’s NMIMS Deemed-to-be University
Mukesh Patel School of Technology Management & Engineering
Text Books
1. E. Horowitz, S. Sahni, Dinesh Mehta, Fundamentals of Data Structures, 2nd edition,
Universities Press, 2008.
2. A. V. Aho, J. E. Hopperoft, J. D. UIlman, Data Structures and Algorithms, Pearson, 1st Ed.,
2006.
3. Reema Thareja, Data Structures Using C- Programming, 3rd edition, Oxford, 2023.
Reference Books
1. Donald E. Knuth, The Art of Computer Programming: Volume 1: Fundamental Algorithms, 3rd
Edition, Pearson Education, 2008.
2. Thomas H. Cormen, Charles E. Leiserson, Ronald L. Rivest, Clifford Stein, Introduction to
Algorithms, The MIT Press.
3. E. Horowitz, S. Sahni, S. A-Freed, Fundamentals of Data Structures, Universities Press, 2008.
4. A. V. Aho, J. E. Hopperoft, J. D. UIlman, Data Structures and Algorithms, Pearson, 1st Ed.,
2006.
Laboratory Work
8 to 10 experiments (and a practicum where applicable) based on the syllabus.
_______________________
Signature
(Program Chairperson)
B. Tech CSE (DS) – 311 (VT)/SEM III/AY 2024-25/Page 17
SVKM’s NMIMS Deemed-to-be University
Mukesh Patel School of Technology Management & Engineering

Semester: III
311 (VT)
````

````text
Course Objective
This course imparts knowledge of data structures and algorithms so as to identify and implement
appropriate data structure and determine the computational complexity of the given application.
Course Outcomes

Unit Description   Duration
1   Introduction   04
Introduction to data structure and its importance, Classification of data structures,
Basic operations., Abstract data type, Performance analysis- time and space
````

## B TECH · Data Science · AY 2023-24 · Sem 3

- Course as printed: Data Structures and Algorithms (code 702CO0C003)
- Source: `04_Syllabi_Archive/B TECH/2ND YEAR/COMPUTER ENGINEERING (CSDS) 311/SEM III/B Tech, CSE Data Science, Sem-III, A Y 2023-24.pdf`, page 16-18

````text
Program: B. Tech Computer Science and Engineering   Semester: III
(Data Science) – 311 (VT)
Course: Data Structures and Algorithms   Code: 702CO0C003
Teaching Scheme   Evaluation Scheme
Lecture   Practical   Tutorial   Internal Continuous   Term End
(Hours   (Hours   (Hours
Credit   Assessment (ICA)   Examinations (TEE)
per   per   per
week)   week)   week)   (Marks - 50)   (Marks - 100)
2   2   0   3   Marks Scaled to 50   Marks Scaled to 50
Pre-requisite: Programming for Problem Solving, Object Oriented programming and
design.
Course Objective
To impart the knowledge of data structures and algorithms so that students can identify
and implement appropriate data structure and determine the computational complexity of
given problem statement.
Course Outcomes
After completion of the course, the student will be able to -
1. Identify suitable data structure for particular type of problem to solve
2. Implement Linear Data Structures
3. Implement Non Linear Data Structure
4. Differentiate among various types of algorithms
Detailed Syllabus
Unit   Description   Duration
1   Introduction to Algorithm & Data Organization   05
Algorithm specification, Recursion, Performance analysis, Asymptotic
Notation - The Big-O, Omega and Theta notation, Data Abstraction,
What is Data structure? Its importance in programming, Ways to
identify the suitable data structure.
2   Linear Data Structure   08
Array, Stack, Queue, Circular Queue, Singly Linked List, Doubly Linked
List, Circular Linked List, Various Representations, Operations &
Applications of Linear Data Structures, Case study of real life problem
to solve using linear data structure.
3   Non-linear Data Structure   09
Trees (Binary Tree, Threaded Binary Tree, Binary Search Tree, B & B+
Tree, AVL Tree, Splay Tree and
Graph: Basic Terminologies and Representations, Graphs (Directed,
Undirected), ,Graph search and traversal algorithms, Operations &
Applications of Non Linear Data Structures, Case study of real life
problem to solve using Nonlinear data structure.
4   Introduction to various Algorithms   08
Greedy Algorithm, Divide and Conquer, Backtracking, Searching,
Sorting, Graph algorithms, Genetic algorithm, Case study of real life
problem to solve using linear data structure.
Total   30
B. Tech CSE (DS) – 311 (VT)/SEM III/AY 2023-24/Page 16
SVKM’s NMIMS Deemed-to-be University
Mukesh Patel School of Technology Management & Engineering
Text Books
1. E. Horowitz, S. Sahni , Dinesh Mehta Fundamentals of Data Structures,2nd edition
Universities Press, 2008.
2. A. V. Aho, J. E. Hopperoft, J. D. UIlman, Data Structures and Algorithms, Pearson,2002.
3. Reema Thareja, Data Structures Using C- Programming, 2nd edition Oxford, , 2014
Reference Books
1. Donald E. Knuth , The Art of Computer Programming: Volume 1: Fundamental Algorithms
2. Thomas, H. Cormen, Charles E. Leiserson, Ronald L. Rivest, Clifford Stein, Introduction
to Algorithms, The MIT Press.
3. E. Horowitz, S. Sahni, S. A-Freed, Fundamentals of Data Structures, Universities Press.
4. A. V. Aho, J. E. Hopperoft, J. D. UIlman, Data Structures and Algorithms, Pearson.
Laboratory Work
8 to 10 experiments (and a practicum where applicable) based on the syllabus.
_______________________
Signature
(Program Chairperson)
B. Tech CSE (DS) – 311 (VT)/SEM III/AY 2023-24/Page 17
SVKM’s NMIMS Deemed-to-be University
Mukesh Patel School of Technology Management & Engineering
Program: B. Tech Computer Science and   Semester: III
Engineering (Data Science) – 311 (VT)
````

- Source: `00_Original_Downloads/SYLLABUS/B TECH/11755__2ND YEAR.zip :: EXTC/SEMESTER III/B_Tech__2nd_Year__(EXTC)__Sem-III__A_Y_2023-24_ZxxTcxvVNA.pdf`, page 11-13

````text
Course: Data Structures and Algorithms   Code: 702CO1C001
Teaching Scheme   Evaluation Scheme
Lecture   Tutorial
Practical   Internal Continuous   Term End Examinations
(Hours   (Hours
(Hours per   Credit   Assessment (ICA)   (TEE)
per   per
week)   (Marks - 50)   (Marks- 100 )
week)   week)
3   2   0   4   Marks Scaled to 50   Marks Scaled to 50
Prerequisite: Programming for Problem Solving
Course Objective
This course imparts knowledge of data structures and algorithms so as to identify and implement
appropriate data structure and determine the computational complexity of the given application.
Course Outcomes
After completion of the course, students will be able to -
1.   Understand the concept of data structures and computational complexity
2.   Identify and implement appropriate linear data structure for the given problem.
3.   Identify and implement appropriate non-linear data structure for the given problem.
4.   Differentiate various searching and sorting algorithms.
Detailed Syllabus
Unit Description   Duration
1   Introduction   04
Introduction to data structure and its importance, Classification of data structures,
Basic operations., Abstract data type, Performance analysis- time and space
complexity, Asymptotic Notations.
2   Linear Data Structure I   10
Representation of arrays in memory, Operations on arrays -Traversal, Insertion,
Deletion. Introduction to Stacks, Operations on Stacks, Applications of stacks -
Expression conversion and evaluation (Polish notation), Balanced parenthesis
checker, Recursion, Introduction to Queue,
Operation on Queues, Linear queue Circular queue, Priority queue, Application
of Queues.
3   Linear Data Structure II   07
Introduction to linked list, Representation of linked list in memory, Singly linked
list and its operations, Introduction to Doubly Linked list Linked list
Signature
(Prepared by Concerned Faculty/HOD)   AY 2023-24
SVKM’s NMIMS University
Mukesh Patel School of Technology Management & Engineering
representation of Stack and Queues, Applications of linked list – Polynomial
Addition
4   Non-Linear Data Structures - I   10
Introduction, Binary tree terminologies, Representation of Binary trees in
memory, Binary Tree traversal algorithms, Construction of Binary Tree from
traversals, Binary Search Tree: Insertion, Deletion, Applications of tree data
structure: Expression trees, Huffman trees.
5   Non- Linear Data Structures - II   06
Introduction, Graph theory terminology, Representation of graph: Adjacency
Matrix, Adjacency List, Graph Traversal: Breadth first search, Depth first search,
Applications of Graphs (Problem Solving): Shortest path (Dijkstra’s algorithm),
Minimum Spanning Tree.
6   Searching and Sorting   08
Linear Search, Binary Search, Selection Sort, Insertion sort, Merge sort,
Introduction to Hashing
Total   45
Text Books
1.   Seymour Lipschutz, “Data structures with C”, Schaum’s Outlines, 1st Edition, 2017.
2.   Reema Thareja, “Data Structures using C”, Oxford University Press, 2nd Edition, 2014.
3.   Y. Langsam, M.J. Augenstein, A.M. Tenenbaum, “Data Structures using C and C++”, PHI 2nd
Edition, 2015.
Reference Books
1.   Richard F. Gillberg, Behrouz A. Forouzen, “Data Structures – A Pseudo Approach with C”,
Cengage Publication, 2nd Edition 2004. (Classic)
2.   Mark Allen Weiss, “Data Structures and Algorithm analysis in C++”, PHI, 4th Edition, 2013.
3.   Thomas, H. Cormen, Charles E. Leiserson, Ronald L. Rivest, Clifford Stein, “Introduction to
Algorithms”, MIT Press, 3rd Edition 2009.
Laboratory Work
8 to 10 experiments (and a practicum where applicable) based on the syllabus.
Signature
(Prepared by Concerned Faculty/HOD)   AY 2023-24
SVKM’s NMIMS Deemed-to-be University
Mukesh Patel School of Technology Management and Engineering

Program: B Tech All Program [except CSBS and CSE(DS) 311 Semester: III /VII
(VT)], MBA Tech All Program,
B Tech Integrated Mechanical, Computer
````

## B TECH INTEGRATED · Computer Engineering · batch 2023-2029 · Sem 7

- Course as printed: Data Structures and Algorithms (code 701CO0C028)
- Source: `04_Syllabi_Archive/B TECH INTEGRATED/Computer/Batch ( 2023 To 2029 )/Semester - 7.pdf`, page 5-8

````text
Program: BTI (CE, IT, DS)   Semester: VII
Course: Data Structures and Algorithms   Code: 701CO0C028
Teaching Scheme   Evaluation Scheme
Lecture   Practical   Tutorial   Internal Continuous   Term End
(Hours   (Hours per (Hours per   Credit   Assessment (ICA)   Examinations (TEE)
per week)   week)   week)   (Marks - 50)   (Marks- 100)
3   0   0   3   Marks Scaled to 50   Marks Scaled to 50
Pre-requisite: Computational Thinking for Problem Solving
Course Objective
This course imparts knowledge of data structures and algorithms so as to identify and implement
appropriate data structure and determine the computational complexity of the given application
Course Outcomes-
After completion of the course, the students will be able to –
1.Apply fundamental data structure concepts and computational complexity (K3)
2.Distinguish between iterative and recursive approaches to problem-solving and implement
algorithms using both techniques (K4)
3.Apply linear data structure and its operations for the given application (K3)
4.Apply non-linear data structure and its operations for the given application (K3)
5.Apply the concept of hashing and hashing functions to create efficient data retrieval mechanisms (K3)
6.Analyze various sorting algorithms (K4)
Detailed Syllabus:
Unit   Description   Duration
1   Introduction   03
Introduction to data structure and its importance, Classification of data structures,
Representation of arrays in memory, Operations on arrays -Traversal, Insertion,
Deletion, Searching (Linear search, Binary search).
Abstract data type, Performance analysis- time and space complexity, Asymptotic   03
Notations.
2   Iteration and Recursion   04
Iterative and recursive algorithms (Towers of Hanoi, Euclid’s GCD algorithm,
Fibonacci series, etc)
3   Linear Data Structure - I   03
Introduction to Stacks, Operations on Stacks, Applications of stacks -
Expression conversion and evaluation (Polish notation), Balanced parenthesis
checker,

queue, Application of Queues.

Introduction to linked list, Representation of linked list in memory, Singly linked list
and its operations, Doubly Linked list and its operations
Linked list representation of Stack and Queues, Applications of linked list –   02
Polynomial Addition. Analysis of algorithms.
5   Hashing   03
Hashing: hashing function,
Collision handling by open addressing, chaining.   02
6   Non-Linear Data Structures- II   04
Introduction, Binary tree terminologies, Representation of Binary trees in memory,
Binary Tree traversal algorithms, Construction of Binary Tree from traversals,
Binary Search Tree: Insertion, Deletion.
AVL trees: Rotations, Insertion. Applications of tree data structure: Heapify, Heap   03
Sort, Huffman trees. Analysis of algorithms.
7   Non- Linear Data Structures - II   03
Introduction, Graph theory terminology, Representation of graph: Adjacency
Matrix, Adjacency List, Graph Traversal: Breadth first search, Depth first search.
Applications of Graphs (Problem Solving): Shortest path (Dijkstra’s algorithm),   03
Minimum Spanning Tree: Prim's and Kruskal's.
Analysis of algorithms.
8   Sorting   03
Insertion, Selection,
Merge Sort, Analysis of algorithms.   02
Total   45
Text Text Books Books
1. Seymour Lipschutz, “Data structures with C”, Schaum’s Outlines, 1st Edition, 2018. (Classic)
2. Reema Thareja, “Data Structures using C”, Oxford University Press, 2nd Edition, 2023
Reference Books:
1. Richard F. Gillberg, Behrouz A. Forouzen, “Data Structures – A Pseudo Approach with C”, Cengage
Publication, 2nd Edition 2004. (Classic)
2. Mark Allen Weiss, “Data Structures and Algorithm analysis in C++”, PHI, 4th Edition, 2013.
3. Thomas, H. Cormen, Charles E. Leiserson, Ronald L. Rivest, Clifford Stein, “Introduction to
Algorithms”, MIT Press, 3rd Edition 2009.
````

````text
This course imparts knowledge of data structures and algorithms so as to identify and implement
````

````text
Unit Description   Duration

Introduction to data structure and its importance, Classification of data structures,

complexity, Asymptotic Notations.
````

- Course as printed: Data Structures & Algorithms (code 702CO0C004)

````text
Program: B. Tech. Computer Science and Business Systems   Semester: II
Course:Data Structures & Algorithms   Code: 702CO0C004
Teaching Scheme   Evaluation Scheme
Lecture   Practical   Tutorial   Internal Continuous   Term End
(Hours per   (Hours per   (Hours per   Credit   Assessment (ICA)   Examinations (TEE)
week)   week)   week)   (Marks - 50)   (Marks- 100)
2   2   1   4   Marks Scaled to 50   Marks Scaled to 50
Pre-requisite: Fundamentals of Computer Science
Course Objective
1. To impart knowledge of data structures and algorithms so that students can identify and
implement appropriate data structure and determine the computational complexity of the
given problem
Course Outcomes
After completion of the course, students will be able to-
1. Determine the space and time complexity
2. Identify and implement appropriate linear data structure for the given problem
3. Identify and implement appropriate non-linear data structure for the given problem
4. Differentiate and Implement various searching, sorting algorithms and hashing
Detailed Syllabus
Unit   Description   Duration
1.   Basic Terminologies & Introduction   to Algorithm and Data   04
Organization: Algorithm specification, Recursion, Performance analysis,
Asymptotic Notation   - The Big-O, Omega and Theta notation,
Programming Style, Refinement of Coding - Time-Space Trade Off, Testing,
Data Abstraction
2.   Linear Data Structure: Array, Stack, Queue, Linked-list and its types,   08
Various Representations, Operations & Applications of Linear Data
Structures
3.   Non-linear Data Structure: Trees (Binary Tree, Threaded Binary Tree,   09
Binary Search Tree, B & B+ Tree, AVL Tree, Splay Tree) and Graphs
(Directed, Undirected), Various Representations, Operations (search and
traversal algorithms and complexity analysis) & Applications of Non-
Linear Data Structures
4.   Searching and Sorting on Various Data Structures: Sequential Search,   06
Binary Search, Breadth First Search, Depth First Search, Insertion Sort,
Selection Sort, Shell Sort, Divide and Conquer Sort, Merge Sort, Quick Sort,
Heap Sort, Introduction to Hashing
5.   File: Organisation (Sequential, Direct, Indexed Sequential, Hashed) and   03
Signature
(Head of the Department)
2022-23 / Page 5
SVKM’s NMIMS
Mukesh Patel School of Technology Management and Engineering
various types of accessing schemes.
Total   30
Text Books
1. E. Horowitz and S. Sahni, Fundamentals of Data Structures, Universities Press, 2nd Edition 2008.
2. Alfred V. Aho, John E. Hopperoft, Jeffrey D. UIlman, Data Structures and Algorithms, Addison
Wesley, 1st Edition 1983.
Reference Books
1. Donald E. Knuth,The Art of Computer Programming: Volume 1: Fundamental Algorithms,
Pearson, 3rd Edition 2009.
2. Thomas, H. Cormen, Charles E. Leiserson, Ronald L. Rivest, Clifford Stein, Introduction to
Algorithms, MIT Press, 3rd Edition 2009.
3. Pat Morin, Open Data Structures: An Introduction (Open Paths to Enriched Learning), UBC Press,
31st Edition 2013.
Laboratory Work
8 to 10 experiments based on the syllabus.

8 to 10 Tutorial exercises based on the syllabus.
````

````text
3   2   0   4   Marks Scaled to 50   Marks Scaled to 50
Prerequisite: Programming for Problem Solving
Course Objectives
This course imparts knowledge of data structures and algorithms so as to identify and
implement appropriate data structure and determine the computational complexity of the given
application.
Course Outcomes
1.   Understand the concept of data structures and computational complexity
2.   Identify and implement appropriate linear data structure for the given problem.
3.   Identify and implement appropriate non-linear data structure for the given problem.
4.   Differentiate various searching and sorting algorithms.
Detailed Syllabus
Unit Description   Duration
1   Introduction   04
Introduction to data structure and its importance, Classification of data
structures, Basic operations., Abstract data type, Performance analysis- time
and space complexity, Asymptotic Notations.
2   Linear Data Structure I   10
Representation of arrays in memory, Operations on arrays -Traversal,
Insertion, Deletion. Introduction to Stacks, Operations on Stacks,
Applications of stacks - Expression conversion and evaluation (Polish
notation), Balanced parenthesis checker, Recursion, Introduction to Queue,
Operation on Queues, Linear queue Circular queue, Priority queue,
Application of Queues.
3   Linear Data Structure II   07
Introduction to linked list, Representation of linked list in memory, Singly
linked list and its operations, Introduction to Doubly Linked list Linked list
representation of Stack and Queues, Applications of linked list – Polynomial
Addition
4   Non-Linear Data Structures - I   10
Introduction, Binary tree terminologies, Representation of Binary trees in
memory, Binary Tree traversal algorithms, Construction of Binary Tree from
traversals, Binary Search Tree: Insertion, Deletion, Applications of tree data
structure: Expression trees, Huffman trees.
5   Non- Linear Data Structures - II   06
Introduction, Graph theory terminology, Representation of graph:
Adjacency Matrix, Adjacency List, Graph Traversal: Breadth first search,
MCA / Sem I /AY 22-23 Page 7
SVKM’s Narsee Monjee Institute of Management Studies
Mukesh Patel School of Technology Management & Engineering
Depth first search, Applications of Graphs (Problem Solving): Shortest path
(Dijkstra’s algorithm), Minimum Spanning Tree.
6   Searching and Sorting   08
Linear Search, Binary Search, Selection Sort, Insertion sort, Merge sort,
Introduction to Hashing
Total   45
Text Books
1.   Seymour Lipschutz, “Data structures with C”, Schaum’s Outlines, 1st Edition, 2017.
2.   Reema Thareja, “Data Structures using C”, Oxford University Press, 2nd Edition,, 2014.
3.   Y. Langsam, M.J. Augenstein, A.M. Tenenbaum, “Data Structures using C and C++”, PHI
2nd Edition, 2015.
Reference Books
1.   Richard F. Gillberg, Behrouz A. Forouzen, “Data Structures – A Pseudo Approach with C”,
Cengage Publication, 2nd Edition 2004. (Classic)
2.   Mark Allen Weiss, “Data Structures and Algorithm analysis in C++”, PHI, 4th Edition, 2013.
3.   Thomas, H. Cormen, Charles E. Leiserson, Ronald L. Rivest, Clifford Stein, “Introduction
to Algorithms”, MIT Press, 3rd Edition 2009.
Laboratory Work
8 to 10 experiments (and a practicum where applicable) based on the syllabus.
_______________________

SVKM’s Narsee Monjee Institute of Management Studies
````

````text
Program: B. Tech Computer Science and Engineering   Semester : III
(Cybersecurity)
Course : Data Structures and Algorithms   Module Code: 702CO0C003
Teaching Scheme   Evaluation Scheme
Lecture   Practical   Tutorial   Internal   Term End

2   2   0   3   Marks Scaled to 50   Marks Scaled to 50
Pre-requisite: Programming for Problem Solving.
Objectives
Enable students to understand linear data structures, implement them to utilize computer
memory efficiently. Also to understand searching and sorting algorithms to solve real-life
problems.
Course Outcomes
After completion of the course, students would be able to-
1. Comprehend data structures and implement stacks and queues,
2. Use linked list to solve real life problem statements,
3. Solve real life problem statements using searching and sorting algorithms.
Detailed Syllabus
Unit   Description   Duration
(Hrs)
1   Introduction to Data Structures: Basic data types, data structure definition,   3
need for data structures, elementary data structure organization,
classification of data structures, operations on data structures, abstract data
type.
2   Linear Data Structure I Arrays and Stacks: Introduction, declaration of   8
arrays, operations on arrays. introduction to stacks, array representation of
stacks, operations of a stack, push, pop, peek, stack overflow and stack
underflow, applications of a stack reversing a list, decimal to binary
conversion using stack, conversion and evaluation of arithmetic
expression, recursion, tower of Hanoi.
3   Linear Data Structure II Queues: Introduction to queues, array   6
representation of queues, queue operations insertion, deletion, queue
overflow, queue underflow, circular queues, priority queues, applications
of queues.
4   Linear Data Structure III Linked List: Introduction, basic terminologies,   8
linked list versus arrays, dynamic memory allocation and deallocation for
a linked list, types of linked lists singly linked list, doubly linked list,
operations of linked list, traversing a linked list, applications of linked lists.
5   Searching and Sorting: Basic searching techniques sequential search,   5
binary search, sorting definitions, selection sort, insertion sort, radix sort,
hashing and collision resolution techniques.
````

## B TECH INTEGRATED · Information Technology · batch 2021-2027 · Sem 7

- Course as printed: Data Structures and Algorithms (code 702CO1C001)
- Source: `04_Syllabi_Archive/B TECH INTEGRATED/IT/Batch ( 2021 To 2027 )/Semester -7.pdf`, page 9-11

````text
Course: Data Structures and Algorithms   Code: 702CO1C001
Teaching Scheme   Evaluation Scheme
Lecture   Tutorial
Practical   Internal Continuous   Term End Examinations
(Hours   (Hours
(Hours per   Credit   Assessment (ICA)   (TEE)
per   per
week)   (Marks - 50)   (Marks- 100 )
week)   week)
3   2   0   4   Marks Scaled to 50   Marks Scaled to 50
Prerequisite: Programming for Problem Solving
Course Objective
This course imparts knowledge of data structures and algorithms so as to identify and implement
appropriate data structure and determine the computational complexity of the given application.
Course Outcomes
After completion of the course, students will be able to -
1.   Understand the concept of data structures and computational complexity
2.   Identify and implement appropriate linear data structure for the given problem.
3.   Identify and implement appropriate non-linear data structure for the given problem.
4.   Differentiate various searching and sorting algorithms.
Detailed Syllabus
Unit Description   Duration
1   Introduction   04
Introduction to data structure and its importance, Classification of data structures,
Basic operations., Abstract data type, Performance analysis- time and space
complexity, Asymptotic Notations.
2   Linear Data Structure I   10
Representation of arrays in memory, Operations on arrays -Traversal, Insertion,
Deletion. Introduction to Stacks, Operations on Stacks, Applications of stacks -
Expression conversion and evaluation (Polish notation), Balanced parenthesis
checker, Recursion, Introduction to Queue,
Operation on Queues, Linear queue Circular queue, Priority queue, Application
of Queues.
3   Linear Data Structure II   07
Introduction to linked list, Representation of linked list in memory, Singly linked
list and its operations, Introduction to Doubly Linked list Linked list
Signature
(Prepared by Concerned Faculty/HOD)   AY 2024-25
SVKM’s NMIMS University
Mukesh Patel School of Technology Management & Engineering
representation of Stack and Queues, Applications of linked list – Polynomial
Addition
4   Non-Linear Data Structures - I   10
Introduction, Binary tree terminologies, Representation of Binary trees in
memory, Binary Tree traversal algorithms, Construction of Binary Tree from
traversals, Binary Search Tree: Insertion, Deletion, Applications of tree data
structure: Expression trees, Huffman trees.
5   Non- Linear Data Structures - II   06
Introduction, Graph theory terminology, Representation of graph: Adjacency
Matrix, Adjacency List, Graph Traversal: Breadth first search, Depth first search,
Applications of Graphs (Problem Solving): Shortest path (Dijkstra’s algorithm),
Minimum Spanning Tree.
6   Searching and Sorting   08
Linear Search, Binary Search, Selection Sort, Insertion sort, Merge sort,
Introduction to Hashing
Total   45
Text Books
1. Seymour Lipschutz, “Data structures with C”, Schaum’s Outlines, 1st Edition, 2017. ISBN-13 : 978-
0070701984.
2. Reema Thareja, “Data Structures using C”, Oxford University Press, 3rd Edition, 2023.
3. Y. Langsam, M.J. Augenstein, A.M. Tenenbaum, “Data Structures using C and C++”, PHI 2nd
Edition, Pearson Education, 2015. ISBN 9789332549319
Reference Books
1. Richard F. Gillberg, Behrouz A. Forouzen, “Data Structures – A Pseudo Approach with C”, Cengage
Publication, 2nd Edition 2004. (Classic)
2. Mark Allen Weiss, “Data Structures and Algorithm analysis in C++”, PHI, 4th Edition, 2013. ISBN-13:
9780132847377 Data Structures and Algorithm Analysis in C++ (pearson.com)
3.Thomas, H. Cormen, Charles E. Leiserson, Ronald L. Rivest, Clifford Stein, “Introduction to
Algorithms”, MIT Press, 3rd Edition 2009.
Laboratory Work
8 to 10 experiments (and a practicum where applicable) based on the syllabus.
Signature
(Prepared by Concerned Faculty/HOD)   AY 2024-25
````

````text
Pre-requisite: Programming for Problem Solving, Object Oriented programming and
design.
Course Objective
To impart the knowledge of data structures and algorithms so that students can identify
and implement appropriate data structure and determine the computational complexity of
given problem statement.
Course Outcomes
After completion of the course, the student will be able to -
1. Identify suitable data structure for particular type of problem to solve
2. Implement Linear Data Structures
3. Implement Non Linear Data Structure
4. Differentiate among various types of algorithms
Detailed Syllabus
Unit   Description   Duration
1   Introduction to Algorithm & Data Organization   05
Algorithm specification, Recursion, Performance analysis, Asymptotic
Notation - The Big-O, Omega and Theta notation, Data Abstraction,
What is Data structure? Its importance in programming, Ways to
identify the suitable data structure.
2   Linear Data Structure   08
Array, Stack, Queue, Circular Queue, Singly Linked List, Doubly Linked
List, Circular Linked List, Various Representations, Operations &
Applications of Linear Data Structures, Case study of real life problem
to solve using linear data structure.
3   Non-linear Data Structure   09
Trees (Binary Tree, Threaded Binary Tree, Binary Search Tree, B & B+
Tree, AVL Tree, Splay Tree and
Graph: Basic Terminologies and Representations, Graphs (Directed,
Undirected), ,Graph search and traversal algorithms, Operations &
Applications of Non Linear Data Structures, Case study of real life
problem to solve using Nonlinear data structure.
4   Introduction to various Algorithms   08
Greedy Algorithm, Divide and Conquer, Backtracking, Searching,
Sorting, Graph algorithms, Genetic algorithm, Case study of real life
problem to solve using linear data structure.
````

## B TECH WORK IN PROCESS · Data Science · batch 2021-2025 · Sem 2

- Course as printed: Data Structures and Algorithms (code 702CO0C003)
- Source: `04_Syllabi_Archive/B TECH WORK IN PROCESS/Data Science/Batch 2021 - 2025/Semester - 2.pdf`, page 11-13

````text
Program: B. Tech. Information Technology, Computer   Semester : II
Engineering, Data Science
Course/Module: Data Structures and Algorithms   Module Code: 702CO0C003
Teaching Scheme   Evaluation Scheme
Practical   Tutorial   Internal   Term End
Lecture
(Hours   (Hours   Continuous   Examinations (TEE)
(Hours per   Credit
per   per   Assessment (ICA)   (Marks- 100
week)
week)   week)   (Marks - 50   )   in Question Paper)
2   2   0   3   Marks Scaled to 50   Marks Scaled to 50
Pre-requisite: Programming for Problem Solving
Course Objectives
Enable students to understand linear data structures, implement them to utilize computer memory
efficiently. Also to understand searching and sorting algorithms to solve real-life problems.
Course Outcomes
After completion of the course, students would be able to
1. comprehend data structures and implement stacks and queues,
2. use linked list to solve real life problem statements,
3. solve real life problem statements using searching and sorting algorithms.
Detailed Syllabus: (per session plan)
Unit   Description   Duration
1.   Introduction to Data Structures: Basic data types, Data structure definition,   3
Need for data structures, Elementary Data Structure organization, Classification
of Data Structures, Operations on Data Structures, Abstract data type.
2.   Linear Data Structure I: Arrays and Stacks: Introduction, Declaration of Arrays,   8
operations on Arrays. Introduction to Stacks, Array representation of Stacks,
Operations of a Stack: Push, Pop, Peek, Stack overflow and Stack underflow,
Applications of a Stack: Reversing a list, Decimal to Binary conversion using
Stack, Conversion and evaluation of Arithmetic Expression, Recursion, Tower
of Hanoi
3.   Linear Data Structure II: Queues: Introduction to Queues, Array representation   6
of Queues, Queue operations: Insertion, Deletion, Queue overflow, Queue
underflow, Circular Queues, Priority Queues, Applications of Queues
4.   Linear Data Structure III: Linked List: Introduction, Basic terminologies,   8
Linked List versus Arrays, Dynamic Memory Allocation and Deallocation for a
Linked List, Types of Linked Lists: Singly Linked List, Doubly Linked List,
Operations of Linked List, Traversing a Linked List, Applications of Linked
Lists.

search, Sorting definitions, Selection Sort, Insertion Sort, Radix Sort, Hashing
and collision resolution techniques

1. Y. Langsam, M.J. Augenstein, A.M. Tenenbaum, “Data Structures using C and C++”, PHI
2nd Edition, 2015.
2. Seymour Lipschutz, “Data structures with C”, Schaum’s Outlines, 1st Edition, 2017.
Reference Books
1. Mark Allen Weiss, PHI publication, “Data Structures and Algorithm analysis in C, PHI
publication, ++”, 4th Edition 2013.
2. Reema Thareja, “Data Structures using C”, 2nd Edition, Oxford University Press, Tata
McGraw Hill, 2014.
````

````text
3   2   0   4   Scaled to 50 marks   Marks Scaled to 50
Pre-requisite- Object Oriented Programming
Objectives- The course will introduce students to both data structures and algorithm
design. The course is designed to introduce different data structures to represent real-
world problems and to study various ways to design algorithms to solve the problems
Course Outcomes-After completion of the course, students will be able to
1. Implement Linear data structures,
2. Implement Non-Linear data structures,
3. Implement and analyze problems of divide and conquer and greedy techniques,
4. Implement and analyze problems of dynamic programming and backtracking
techniques.
Detailed Syllabus: (per session plan)
Unit   Description   Duration
1.   Linear Data Structures I:   07
Types of Data Structures, Abstract data type, Arrays, Structure,
Stacks:   Array representation of Stacks, Stack Operations,
Applications of a Stack: Reversing a list, Conversion and Evaluation
of Arithmetic Expression, Recursion Queues: Array representation of
Queues, Queue operations: Types of Queues: Simple Queues,
Circular Queues, Priority Queues
2.   Linear Data Structures II - Linked List:   04
Linked List versus Arrays, Memory Allocation and Deallocation for a
Linked List, Singly Linked List Operations, Applications of Linked
Lists.
3.   Non-Linear Data Structures - Trees:   07
Binary tree terminologies, Representation of Binary trees in memory,
Binary Tree traversal algorithms: Inorder, Preorder, Postorder,
Applications of tree data structures. Graphs: Introduction, Graph
theory terminology, Representation of graph: Adjacency Matrix,
Adjacency List, Graph Traversal: Breadth first search, Depth first
search
MCA/ Sem II /A.Y. 2021-22/ Page 3
SVKM’s NMIMS
Mukesh Patel School of Technology Management & Engineering
4.   Analyzing the Algorithms:   04
Time and space Complexity, Asymptotic notations, Asymptotic
order, Properties of big oh, big omega, and big theta Classifying
functions by their asymptotic growth rates, Asymptotic order of
common problems. Master’Theorm.
5.   Divide and Conquer Technique:   06
The general method, Control abstraction for divide and conquer,
Finding the maximum and minimum: straightforward and recursive
algorithm, Binary Search Merge sort, Quick sort, Time complexity
Analysis.
6.   Greedy Technique:   06
The general method, control abstraction, Fractional Knapsack
problem, Minimum spanning trees, Prim’s and Kruskal’s algorithm,
Shortest Path using Dijkstra’s algorithm, Huffman Codes. Time
Complexity Analysis
7.   Dynamic Programming:   08
The general method, principle of optimality, All pairs shortest paths -
Floyds’ Warshall Algorithm, Longest common sequence, 0/1-
knapsack, The travelling salesperson problem. Time complexity
Analysis.
8. Backtracking:   03
The general method, The n–queens’ problem, Sum of subsets. Time
Complexity Analysis. Introduction to NP, NP hard and NP complete
problems.
Total   45
Text Books:
1. Reema Thareja, “Data Structures using C”, 2nd edition, Oxford University Press,
2014.
2.   Ellis Horowitz, and Sartaj Sahani, “Fundamentals of Computer Algorithms”, 2nd
edition, University Press, 2008.
3. Thomas H. Cormen, Charles E., Leiserson, Ronald L. Rivest, “Introduction to
Algorithms”, 3rd edition, MIT Press, 2009.
Reference Books:
1. P. H. Dava and H.B. Dava; “Design and Anlaysis of Algorithms‖”, Pearson
Education, 2008.
2. Alan Van Gelder, “Computer Algorithms Introduction to Design and Analysis”,
3rd Edition,Addison-Wesley,2000.
3. Aho, Hopcroft, Ullman, “Data Structures and Algorithms”, Addison-Wesley,
2000.
````

````text
Prerequisite: Programming for Problem Solving
Course Objective
This course imparts knowledge of data structures and algorithms so as to identify and implement
appropriate data structure and determine the computational complexity of the given application.
Course Outcomes
After completion of the course, students will be able to -
1.   Understand the concept of data structures and computational complexity
2.   Identify and implement appropriate linear data structure for the given problem.
3.   Identify and implement appropriate non-linear data structure for the given problem.
4.   Differentiate various searching and sorting algorithms.
Detailed Syllabus
Unit Description   Duration
1   Introduction   04
Introduction to data structure and its importance, Classification of data structures,
Basic operations., Abstract data type, Performance analysis- time and space
complexity, Asymptotic Notations.
2   Linear Data Structure I   10
Representation of arrays in memory, Operations on arrays -Traversal, Insertion,
Deletion. Introduction to Stacks, Operations on Stacks, Applications of stacks -
Expression conversion and evaluation (Polish notation), Balanced parenthesis
checker, Recursion, Introduction to Queue,
Operation on Queues, Linear queue Circular queue, Priority queue, Application
of Queues.
3   Linear Data Structure II   07
Introduction to linked list, Representation of linked list in memory, Singly linked
list and its operations, Introduction to Doubly Linked list Linked list

representation of Stack and Queues, Applications of linked list – Polynomial
Addition
4   Non-Linear Data Structures - I   10
Introduction, Binary tree terminologies, Representation of Binary trees in
memory, Binary Tree traversal algorithms, Construction of Binary Tree from
traversals, Binary Search Tree: Insertion, Deletion, Applications of tree data
structure: Expression trees, Huffman trees.
5   Non- Linear Data Structures - II   06
Introduction, Graph theory terminology, Representation of graph: Adjacency
Matrix, Adjacency List, Graph Traversal: Breadth first search, Depth first search,
Applications of Graphs (Problem Solving): Shortest path (Dijkstra’s algorithm),
Minimum Spanning Tree.
6   Searching and Sorting   08
Linear Search, Binary Search, Selection Sort, Insertion sort, Merge sort,
Introduction to Hashing
Total   45

3.   Y. Langsam, M.J. Augenstein, A.M. Tenenbaum, “Data Structures using C and C++”, PHI 2nd
Edition, 2015.
````

````text
Pre-requisite: Fundamentals of Computer Science

Course Objectives:
1. To impart knowledge of data structures and algorithms so that students can
identify and implement appropriate data structure and determine the
computational complexity of the given problem.

Course Outcomes:
After completion of the course, students would be able to:
1. Determine the space and time complexity.
2. Identify and implement appropriate linear data structure for the given problem.
3. Identify and implement appropriate non-linear data structure for the given
problem.
4, Differentiate and Implement various searching, sorting algorithms and hashing.

Detailed Syllabus:

Unit | Description Duration

1 Basic Terminologies & Introduction to Algorithm and Data 4
Organization: Algorithm specification, Recursion, Performance
analysis, Asymptotic Notation - The Big-O, Omega and Theta
notation, Programming Style, Refinement of Coding - Time-Space
Trade Off, Testing, Data Abstraction

2. Linear Data Structure: Array, Stack, Queue, Linked-list and its types, 8
Various Representations, Operations & Applications of Linear Data
Structures

3. Non-linear Data Structure: Trees (Binary Tree, Threaded Binary Tree, 9
Binary Search Tree, B & B+ Tree, AVL Tree, Splay Tree) and Graphs
(Directed, Undirected), Various Representations, Operations (search
and traversal algorithms and complexity analysis) & Applications of
Non-Linear Data Structures

4. Searching and Sorting on Various Data Structures: Sequential 6
Search, Binary Search, Breadth First Search, Depth First Search,
Insertion Sort, Selection Sort, Shell Sort, Divide and Conquer Sort,
````

````text
1. Understand the concepts of data structure and their basic usability in different
applications.

2. Implement various data structure such as stacks, queues, trees and graphs to solve
various computing problems.

3. Analyze various searching and sorting algorithms based on their time complexity.

approach used to solve real world problems.

Unit | Description Duration

of Files,Operations with files, types of files.

Insertion Deletion, Traversing, Searching, Types of Linked list, Garbage Collection,

tree traversals, Types of Binary Trees: Left & Right skewed, Complete, Extended,
````

````text
2. To discuss different data structures to represent real world problems

3. To study various ways to design algorithms to solve the problems

Course Outcomes: After completion of the course, students will be able to:

1. Classify Linear and nonlinear Data Structures and Implement its applications

2. Analyze and Differentiate between searching and sorting techniques with respect to
efficiency

3. Discuss Complexity Theory for analysis of algorithm

4. Compare and Contrast different algorithm design strategies

5. Formulate a problem and develop an algorithmic strategy for a case study

Detailed Syllabus: (per session plan)

Unit | Description Duration

1. Introduction to Data Structures: Types of Data Structures, Abstract 6
data type, Arrays, Structure.

2. Introduction to algorithm analysis and design Strategies: Divide & 6
Conquer, Greedy Technique, Dynamic Programming and Backtracking.

3. Linear Data Structures and Recursion: Stacks: Operations on Stack, 8

Applications, Queue: Linear, Circular and Priority Queue with its
Operations and Application, Linked List: Singly, Doubly and Circular
Linked List with its operations and Application, Recursion: Definition,
its processing , efficiency and difference between iterative approach and
Recursive approach

4. Searching and Sorting Techniques: Efficiency considerations in 8
searching, Basic Searching Techniques- Sequential search, Indexed
sequential search, Binary search, Interpolation search, Hashing.
Efficiency considerations in sorting, General sorting Methods- Bubble
sort, Insertion Sort, Selection Sort, Heaps and Heap sort, Merge sort,

Quick sort, complexity analysis, Best case, worst case and average case.

techniques, Binary Search Tree with its operations, AVL Trees, Multi-
way Search tree, Expression Tree, Threaded Binary Tree and Game Tree

6. Graphs: Terminologies, Memory representation of grapTraversal 7
techniques(Breadth First Search and Depth First Search), Minimum
spanning trees, Prim’s and Kruskal’s algorithm, Single source shortest
path and All pair shortest path

Total 45
````

````text
Objectives:

1. To introduce students to both data structures and algorithm design.
2. To discuss different data structures to represent real world problems
3. To study various ways to design algorithms to solve the problems

Course Outcomes: After completion of the course, students will be able to:
1. Explain basic concepts of data structures and analysis of algorithms.
2. Implement Search and traversal algorithms
3. Develop algorithms for various sorting techniques.
4, Analyze Time and Space complexity various search and sort algorithms
5. Apply greedy technique, backtracking, and dynamic programming to solve real

Unit | Description Duration

Postfix, Prefix; Queues-Circular, Priority Queue, Deque ; Trees-Binary
Tree, and Graphs.

and Space Complexity, Lower bounds and the complexity of problems,
Classifying functions by their asymptotic growth rates, Different study
area’s in algorithm, Asymptotic notations, Asymptotic order,
Properties of big oh, big omega, and big theta, Asymptotic order of
common problems, Best case, average case and worst case analysis for
common problems, Optimality.

order, Preorder and Post-order tree traversal. Search and traversal
techniques for graphs - BFS, DFS.

searching, Basic Searching Techniques- Sequential search, Searching
ordered table, Indexed sequential search, Binary search, Interpolation
search, Binary search tree, Hashing. Efficiency considerations in sorting,

sort, Heap as Priority queues, Shell sort, Bucket sort, Radix sort. Time

complexity calculations, Best case, worst case and average case

control abstraction, Merge sort, Insertion sort, Quick sort, Selection sort,

Time complexity calculations, Best case, worst case and average case
calculations of the different sorting methods.

6. Greedy Technique: The general method, Knapsack problem, Minimum 5
spanning trees, Prim’s and Kruskal’s algorithm, Single source shortest

paths algorithm.
7. Dynamic Programming: The general method, principle of optimality, 5
All pairs shortest paths, Optimal binary search trees, 0/1-knapsack.

backtracking, The Tic-Tac-Toe problem, Knapsack problem.
````

````text
Pre-requisite: Basic Electrical Engineering (BTAB01003), Basic Electronics (BTAB02003)

Objective: To provide knowledge of logic circuits theory, elementary analysis and its
implementation in practical cases; the popular logic families and their characteristics that
will help to understand design of complex digital circuits and systems.

Outcomes:

After successful completion of this course, students should be able to:

¢ Solve basic binary math operations using the logic gates.

¢« Demonstrate programming proficiency using the various logical elements to
design practically motivated logical units,

¢ Design different units that are elements of typical computer’s CPU.

¢ Apply knowledge of the logic design course to solve problems of designing of
control units of different input/output devices.

e Design electrical circuitry using logical elements realized on the base of different

technologies.
Detailed Syllabus:
Unit | Description Duration
1 | Number Systems: Decimal, Binary, Octal and Hexadecimal number 3

system and conversion, Binary weighted codes, Signed number
binary order, 1's and 2's complement codes, Binary arithmetic.

2 | Boolean Algebra: Binary logic functions, Boolean laws, Truth tables, 5
Associative and distributive properties, DeMorgan’s Theorems,
Realization of switching functions using logic gates.

3 | Combinational Logic : Switching equations, Canonical logic forms, 8
Sum of product & Product of sums, Karnaugh maps, Two, Three and
Four variable Karnaugh maps, Simplification of expressions, Quine-
McCluskey minimization techniques, Mixed logic combinational
circuits, Multiple output functions.

4 | Analysis and Design of Combinational Logic : Introduction to 8
combinational circuit, Code conversion, Decoder, Encoder, Priority
encoder, Multiplexers as function generators, Binary address,
Subtractor, BCD adder, Binary comparator, Arithmetic and logic
units. aaa

considerations.

6 | Sequential Circuits: State diagrams and tables, Transition table, 8
Excitation table and equations, Examples using flip-flops. Simple

Construction of state diagram and counter design.

7 | Programmable Logic: Programmable logic devices, Programmable 5
logic arrays and programmable array logic, Design using PAL, Field

Implementation of Atomicity and Durability, Concurrent
Executions, Serializability, Testing for — Serializability,
Recoverability, Implementation of Isolation, Transaction in SOL.

8, Concurrency control and recovery: Lock-Based Protocols, 5
Timestamp-Based Protocols, Validation-Based Protocols, Failure
Classification, Storage Structure, Recovery and Atomicity, Log-

Based Recovery, Shadow Paging

Management System, Banking System, Inventory System.
````
